## Comprehensive Codebase Analysis: Claude Agent SDK & LLM Configuration

Based on my thorough exploration of the DuckDB Data Agent project, here's a detailed technical report:

---

### 1. CLAUDE AGENT SDK USAGE

#### Python Backend (agent.py)

**Import & Usage:**
```python
from claude_agent_sdk import AgentDefinition
```

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/agent.py`

**Key Integration Points:**

- **Subagent Definitions** (lines 63-125): Two `AgentDefinition` objects are created for the subagent architecture:
  - **sql-analyst**: Executes SQL queries, has access to `mcp__duckdb__execute_sql` tool
  - **chart-builder**: Creates visualizations, has access to `mcp__duckdb__execute_sql` and `mcp__duckdb__render_chart` tools

- **Model Configuration** (lines 115, 123):
  ```python
  model=SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL,      # defaults to "haiku"
  model=CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL,    # defaults to "haiku"
  ```

- **Agent Execution via Container** (lines 164-520):
  - `stream_chat()` function orchestrates the agent lifecycle
  - Spawns Docker sidecar containers via `container_manager.create()`
  - Passes agent definitions to container via JSON payload (line 261-269)
  - Streams agent responses from container as SSE events
  - Extracts subagent text/chart specs from SDK message streams

#### TypeScript Sidecar (server.ts)

**Import & Usage:**
```typescript
import { query, AgentDefinition } from "@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk";
```

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/sidecar/src/server.ts`

**Key Integration Points:**

- **SDK Query Initialization** (lines 190-219):
  ```typescript
  const sdkQuery = query({
    prompt: body.message,
    options: {
      model: modelName,
      systemPrompt: body.system_prompt,
      allowedTools: ["Task", "mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
      permissionMode: "bypassPermissions",
      allowDangerouslySkipPermissions: true,
      maxTurns: 20,
      includePartialMessages: true,  // Enable token-level streaming
      abortController,
      env: sdkEnv,
      agents: sdkAgents,  // Subagent definitions passed from backend
      mcpServers: { duckdb: { type: "sse", url: body.mcp_server_url } },
      resume: body.session_id,  // Resume previous sessions
    },
  });
  ```

- **Agent Definition Conversion** (lines 176-188):
  ```typescript
  // Backend sends agents payload; convert to SDK AgentDefinition format
  for (const [name, def] of Object.entries(body.agents)) {
    sdkAgents[name] = {
      description: def.description,
      prompt: def.prompt,
      tools: def.tools,
      model: def.model,
    };
  }
  ```

- **Message Streaming** (lines 233-314):
  - Iterates over SDK query results with `for await (const message of sdkQuery)`
  - Streams each message as SSE data events to frontend
  - Handles token usage tracking, Langfuse tracing, and abort signals

- **Idle Timeout Protection** (lines 225-239):
  - 60-second idle timeout resets on every message
  - Prevents hanging on broken MCP or API connections

#### Tracing Integration (tracing.py)

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/tracing.py`

**LangSmith Auto-Instrumentation:**
```python

if configure_claude_agent_sdk():
    logger.info("Claude Agent SDK auto-instrumentation enabled")
```

### 2. LLM API CONFIGURATION

#### Environment Variables (config.py)

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/config.py`

```python

**Defaults:**
- **Orchestrator Model**: `claude-sonnet-4-6` (powerful, used for agent coordination)
- **SQL Subagent**: `haiku` (fast, cost-effective)
- **Chart Subagent**: `haiku` (fast, cost-effective)

#### API Key Handling: Credential Proxy Pattern

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/proxy.py`

**Architecture:**
```
Sidecar Container (UUID token)
  ↓ ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://host:8000/anthropic
  ↓ ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=<uuid>
FastAPI Proxy Router (/anthropic/{path})
  ↓ Validates token
  ↓ Swaps UUID for real ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
Anthropic API (api.anthropic.com)
```

**Token Management:**
```python
class ProxyTokenStore:
    def create_token(self) -> str:
        token = str(uuid.uuid4())  # Short-lived UUID
        self._tokens[token] = datetime.now(timezone.utc) + timedelta(seconds=self._ttl)
        return token
    
    def validate_token(self, token: str) -> bool:
        # Multi-use token for entire session (600s TTL)
        # Explicitly revoked when session ends
        
    def revoke_token(self, token: str) -> None:
        # Called in agent.py's finally block (line 533)
```

**Token Lifecycle** (agent.py, lines 185-534):
1. Backend mints token before session: `session_token=[REDACTED].create_token()`
2. Token injected into container: `"ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": session_token`
3. Real key injected into proxy: `headers["x-api-key"] = ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`
4. Token revoked after session: `proxy_token_store.revoke_token(session_token)` (10s delay)

**Request Flow** (proxy.py, lines 73-134):
```python
@router.api_route("/{path:path}", methods=["GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "PATCH"])
async def proxy_anthropic(path: str, request: Request):
    # Validate session token (skip for model discovery endpoints)
    session_token = request.headers.get("x-api-key") or request.headers.get("authorization")
    if not session_token or not proxy_token_store.validate_token(session_token):
        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid or expired session token")
    
    # Swap token for real key
    headers["x-api-key"] = ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
    headers["anthropic-version"] = "2023-06-01"
    
    # Forward to Anthropic upstream
    upstream_resp = await client.send(upstream_req, stream=True)
```

### 3. DOCKER-COMPOSE SETUP

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/docker-compose.yml`

**Services:**

```yaml
services:
  app:
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: backend/Dockerfile
    image: duckdb-data-agent:latest
    ports:
      - "${APP_PORT:-10000}:10000"
    env_file:
      - backend/.env  # Loads ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, model configs
    environment:
      CONTAINER_IMAGE: duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest
      CONTAINER_NETWORK: agent-sandbox
      PROXY_BASE_URL: http://duckdb-data-agent:10000  # For container-to-host communication
    volumes:
      - /var/run/docker.sock:/var/run/docker.sock  # Docker SDK access
    networks:
      - agent-sandbox
    restart: unless-stopped

  sidecar:
    build:
      context: ./sidecar
      dockerfile: Dockerfile
    image: duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest
    profiles:
      - sidecar
    # Never started as service — app spawns containers on-demand

networks:
  agent-sandbox:
    name: agent-sandbox
    external: true
```

**Backend Dockerfile:**
- Python 3.12 slim base
- FastAPI + Uvicorn
- Node.js 20 (for frontend build)
- No Claude CLI needed (sidecar handles agent execution)

**Sidecar Dockerfile:**
- Python 3.12 slim + Node.js 20
- `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk` (npm dependency)
- `@anthropic-ai/claude-code` CLI globally installed
- Express.js HTTP server
- TypeScript compilation

#### Credential Proxy (/anthropic)

**In main.py (line 70):**

```

**Unauthenticated Passthrough** (proxy.py, lines 70-71):

```

#### MCP SSE Bridge (/mcp/sse)

**In main.py (line 73):**
```python
app.mount("/mcp", mcp_app)
```

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/mcp_sse.py`

**Features:**

- Session-based routing via `session_id` query parameter
- Tools: `execute_sql`, `ask_user_question`, `render_chart`
- Handles DuckDB SQL execution and chart rendering

### 5. ARCHITECTURE: AGENT MODE VS CONTAINER MODE

#### Agent Mode (Local SDK)

**Status:** Fully implemented (container mode is primary; agent mode may be legacy)

**Flow:**
1. Frontend sends chat message
2. Backend imports Claude Agent SDK (Python)
3. Executes agent locally as subprocess
4. Streams responses to frontend

**Current Implementation:** Used in older code paths (not primary in this codebase)

#### Container Mode (Sidecar Sandbox)

**Status:** Production primary mode

**Flow:**

```
Frontend Chat Request
  ↓
FastAPI Backend (/api/chat)
  ├─ Create session token (UUID)
  ├─ Spin up gVisor sidecar container (or reuse)
  └─ POST /query with:
     - message + system_prompt
     - agent definitions (sql-analyst, chart-builder)
     - model name (claude-sonnet-4-6)
     - MCP SSE URL for DuckDB
     - proxy token + base URL
        ↓
Sidecar Container (gVisor Sandbox)
  ├─ Claude Agent SDK query()
  ├─ Calls Claude API via proxy
  │  ├─ ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=<uuid token>
  │  ├─ ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://host:8000/anthropic
  │  └─ Resolved via proxy: uuid → real key
  ├─ Tool calls to DuckDB via MCP SSE
  │  ├─ /mcp/sse?session_id=...
  │  └─ Routed to per-user DuckDB instance
  └─ Streams SSE events back to backend
        ↓
FastAPI Backend
  ├─ Parses SDK stream events
  ├─ Extracts subagent chart specs
  ├─ Forwards to frontend as SSE
  └─ Revokes proxy token (10s delay)
        ↓
Frontend
  ├─ Renders text responses
  ├─ Renders charts from spec
  └─ Displays tool calls + results
```

**Container Isolation** (container_manager.py):

```python
container = self._client.containers.run(
    image="duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest",
    runtime=CONTAINER_RUNTIME,  # "runc" or "runsc" (gVisor)
    mem_limit=CONTAINER_MEMORY_LIMIT,  # "512m"
    nano_cpus=int(CONTAINER_CPU_LIMIT * 1e9),  # "0.5" CPU
    read_only=True,  # Read-only root filesystem
    cap_drop=["ALL"],  # Drop all Linux capabilities
    security_opt=["no-new-privileges"],
    tmpfs={
        "/tmp": "size=50m",
        "/home/appuser": "size=50m,uid=1000,gid=1000"
    },
    ports={"3000/tcp": None},  # Ephemeral host port
    labels={"app": "duckdb-agent-sidecar", "session_id": session_id},
)
```

**Session Reuse** (agent.py, line 211):
```python
stable_session = backend_session_id or session_id or "default"
# Reuse same container across requests from same browser tab
# (Claude SDK session_id changes after each response)
```

### SUMMARY

This is a sophisticated **containerized agent architecture** that:

1. **Uses Claude Agent SDK** via two implementations:
   - Python SDK (`claude-agent-sdk`) in backend for subagent definitions
   - TypeScript SDK (`@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk`) in sidecar for execution

2. **Proxies API Keys** via a credential proxy pattern:
   - Mints session-specific UUID tokens
   - Containers never see real `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`
   - Real key stays server-side in FastAPI proxy

3. **Isolates Agents** in gVisor sandboxed containers:
   - Per-session containers (reused across requests from same tab)
   - Read-only filesystem, capability drop, no Docker socket access
   - MCP SSE bridge routes DuckDB access without direct database access

4. **Models** configurable via env vars:
   - Orchestrator: `claude-sonnet-4-6` (default)
   - Subagents: `haiku` (default, configurable independently)

5. **Observability** integrated:
   - Langfuse tracing (optional)
   - LangSmith OTel auto-instrumentation for SDK
   - Per-turn token usage tracking in sidecar

## Bifrost LLM Gateway/Proxy: Technical Research Summary

### 1. What is Bifrost?

Bifrost is a high-performance, open-source LLM gateway/proxy built in **Go** by **Maxim AI**. It acts as an intermediary layer between client applications and multiple LLM providers, presenting a unified API surface. Its core value proposition is being a drop-in replacement for tools like LiteLLM, with claims of 50x faster performance (adding only ~11 microseconds of overhead per request at 5,000 RPS sustained load). It is licensed under **Apache 2.0**.

- **GitHub**: [github.com/maximhq/bifrost](https://github.com/maximhq/bifrost)
- **Documentation**: [docs.getbifrost.ai](https://docs.getbifrost.ai/)
- **Product page**: [getmaxim.ai/bifrost](https://www.getmaxim.ai/bifrost)

### 2. How It Works as a Multi-Provider Proxy

Bifrost sits between your application and LLM providers. The client sends requests to Bifrost, which then:

1. Receives the request on a unified endpoint
2. Determines the target provider based on the model name prefix (e.g., `openai/gpt-4o-mini`, `anthropic/claude-3-opus`)
3. Translates the request format to the target provider's native API format
4. Handles authentication using its internally-stored API keys
5. Applies load balancing across multiple API keys (weighted distribution)
6. Provides automatic failover if a provider returns errors
7. Translates the response back to the format the client expects
8. Optionally caches responses via semantic caching

### 3. Supported Providers

Bifrost supports **20+ providers**, including:

| Provider | Notes |
|----------|-------|
| **OpenAI** | Direct API |
| **Anthropic** | Direct API |
| **AWS Bedrock** | IAM role or explicit credentials |
| **Google Vertex AI** | Service account + project/region |
| **Azure OpenAI** | Service principal or API key, with deployment mappings |
| **Cerebras** | |
| **Cohere** | |
| **Mistral** | |
| **Groq** | |
| **Ollama** | Self-hosted, requires custom `network_config` with `base_url` |
| **vLLM** | Self-hosted, requires `custom_provider_config` |

### 4. Deployment Options

**NPX (quickest start):**
```bash
npx -y @maximhq/bifrost
```

**Docker (production):**
```bash
docker pull maximhq/bifrost
docker run -p 8080:8080 -v $(pwd)/data:/app/data maximhq/bifrost
```
Architecture-specific tags available: `:v1.3.9-amd64`, `:v1.3.9-arm64`

**Go binary:**
```bash
go install github.com/maximhq/bifrost/transports/bifrost-http@latest
```

**Go SDK (embed in Go application):**
```bash
go get github.com/maximhq/bifrost/core
```

**Configuration flags:**

| Parameter | Default | NPX flag | Docker env var |
|-----------|---------|----------|----------------|
| port | 8080 | `-port 8080` | `APP_PORT=8080` |
| host | localhost | `-host 0.0.0.0` | `APP_HOST=0.0.0.0` |
| log-level | info | `-log-level info` | `LOG_LEVEL=info` |
| log-style | json | `-log-style json` | `LOG_STYLE=json` |

Data directory (stores `config.json`, `config.db` SQLite, `logs.db`): defaults to `~/.config/bifrost`, overridable with `-app-dir`.

### 5. How Clients Connect

Bifrost provides **two types of endpoints**:

**A. Unified OpenAI-compatible endpoint** (`/v1/chat/completions`):
Uses model name prefixes to route to different providers:
```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8080/v1/chat/completions \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "model": "openai/gpt-4o-mini",
    "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "Hello"}]
  }'
```

Model prefix examples:
- `openai/gpt-4o-mini` or just `gpt-4o-mini` -- routes to OpenAI
- `anthropic/claude-3-opus` -- routes to Anthropic
- `vertex/gemini-pro` -- routes to Google Vertex
- `azure/gpt-4o` -- routes to Azure OpenAI
- `ollama/llama3.1:8b` -- routes to local Ollama

**B. Provider-specific native endpoints**:
- `/openai` -- OpenAI-native API format
- `/anthropic` -- Anthropic-native API format (Messages API)
- `/genai` -- Google GenAI format

This means if you have an Anthropic SDK client, you point it to `http://localhost:8080/anthropic` and it works natively without format translation on the client side.

### 6. OpenAI SDK Compatibility

Yes, Bifrost is fully OpenAI-SDK-compatible. You change only the `base_url`:

**Python:**
```python
from openai import OpenAI

client = OpenAI(
    api_key="placeholder-key",  # Bifrost manages real keys
    base_url="http://localhost:8080/openai"
)

response = client.chat.completions.create(
    model="anthropic/claude-3-opus",  # Routes to Anthropic via Bifrost
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Hello"}]
)
```

**JavaScript/TypeScript:**
```javascript
const client = new OpenAI({
    apiKey: "placeholder-key",
    baseURL: "http://localhost:8080/openai"
});
```

Supported features through the OpenAI-compatible interface:
- Chat completions (streaming and non-streaming)
- Vision / multimodal (images, audio)
- Function calling / tool use
- Embeddings
- JSON mode and structured outputs
- Files API and Batch API

### 7. Authentication / API Key Handling

Bifrost centralizes API key management. There are two modes:

**A. Bifrost-managed keys (recommended for production):**
API keys are stored in Bifrost's `config.json` or configured via the Web UI. Client applications use a placeholder/dummy API key. Bifrost injects the real provider key when forwarding the request.

Configuration example (`config.json`):
```json
{
  "providers": {
    "openai": {
      "keys": [
        {
          "name": "openai-key-1",
          "value": "env.OPENAI_API_KEY",
          "models": ["gpt-4o-mini", "gpt-4o"],
          "weight": 0.7
        },
        {
          "name": "openai-key-2",
          "value": "sk-proj-xxxxxxxxx",
          "models": [],
          "weight": 0.3
        }
      ]
    },
    "anthropic": {
      "keys": [
        {
          "name": "anthropic-key-1",
          "value": "env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY",
          "models": [],
          "weight": 1.0
        }
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

Key features:
- `env.VARIABLE_NAME` syntax references environment variables
- `weight` controls load balancing distribution across keys
- `models` can restrict which models a key is used for (empty = all)
- Sensitive values are redacted in UI and GET responses

**B. Direct key passthrough (development/testing):**
When "Allow Direct API keys" is enabled, clients can pass real provider keys in headers and Bifrost forwards them directly.

**Cloud provider authentication:**
- **Azure**: Service principal (client ID/secret/tenant) or API key with deployment mappings
- **AWS Bedrock**: Explicit access key/secret key or IAM role-based auth
- **Google Vertex**: Project ID + region + service account credentials

**Virtual keys (governance):**
Bifrost supports virtual keys for per-team/per-project access control:
```python
client = OpenAI(
    api_key="placeholder",
    base_url="http://localhost:8080/openai",
    default_headers={"x-bf-vk": "team-staging-key"}
)
```
Virtual keys enable independent rate limits, budget controls, and routing policies.

**Secret management integrations:** HashiCorp Vault, AWS Secrets Manager.

### 8. Additional Notable Features

- **Web UI**: Admin dashboard at `http://localhost:8080` for visual configuration, monitoring, and governance
- **Prometheus metrics**: Native `/metrics` endpoint
- **OpenTelemetry/OTLP**: Distributed tracing support
- **Cluster mode**: Multi-node deployments for horizontal scaling
- **Plugin system**: Go and WebAssembly plugins for custom middleware
- **MCP support**: Model Context Protocol tool integration with allow-list filtering
- **Guardrails**: Integration with AWS Bedrock Guardrails and Azure Content Safety

### Comparison to LiteLLM

| Aspect | Bifrost | LiteLLM |
|--------|---------|---------|
| Language | Go | Python |
| Overhead | ~11 microseconds at 5k RPS | Higher (Python runtime) |
| Provider endpoints | Unified + provider-native (`/openai`, `/anthropic`) | Unified OpenAI-compatible |
| Configuration | JSON file + Web UI + API | YAML/env vars + proxy config |
| Key management | Built-in with weighted load balancing | Virtual keys + config |
| Deployment | npx, Docker, Go binary | pip, Docker |

Now let me propose approaches. Based on your answers, here are **3 approaches** with trade-offs:

---

## Approach 1: Bifrost as Drop-in Replacement for Credential Proxy (Recommended)

**How it works:**
- Add Bifrost as a Docker Compose service on the `agent-sandbox` network
- Bifrost manages all provider API keys centrally via `config.json`
- Replace the existing `proxy.py` credential proxy entirely
- Sidecar containers point `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` to `http://bifrost:8080/anthropic`
- No changes to the Claude Agent SDK or sidecar TypeScript code — Bifrost's `/anthropic` endpoint is API-compatible
- Model names use Bifrost's prefix routing (e.g., `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6`, `openai/gpt-4o-mini`)

**Pros:**
- Minimal code changes (sidecar/SDK stay the same)
- Bifrost's `/anthropic` endpoint means native API format — no translation issues
- Clean removal of `proxy.py` and `ProxyTokenStore`
- Get load balancing, failover, and multi-provider for free
- Web UI for monitoring at port 8080

**Cons:**
- New service dependency in the stack
- Need to validate Bifrost handles all Anthropic SDK features (streaming, tool use, beta headers)

---

## Approach 2: Bifrost with OpenAI-Compatible Endpoint

**How it works:**
- Same Docker Compose setup, but route through `/v1/chat/completions` (OpenAI-compatible)
- Would require changing the sidecar to use OpenAI SDK instead of Claude Agent SDK

**Pros:**
- Maximum provider flexibility — any model works identically
- Well-tested OpenAI-compatible codepath in Bifrost

**Cons:**
- **Major rewrite** — must replace Claude Agent SDK with OpenAI-compatible client
- Lose Claude-specific features (extended thinking, prompt caching, tool_use format)
- Contradicts your decision to keep the Claude Agent SDK

---

## Approach 3: Bifrost Behind Existing Proxy

**How it works:**
- Keep `proxy.py` as the token-validation layer
- `proxy.py` forwards to Bifrost instead of `api.anthropic.com`
- Bifrost then routes to the actual provider

**Pros:**
- Preserves existing session token security model
- Lower risk — no changes to sidecar or proxy consumers

**Cons:**
- Three-hop architecture (sidecar → proxy → Bifrost → provider)
- Extra latency and complexity
- Duplicates auth management between proxy and Bifrost

---

**My recommendation: Approach 1.** It's the cleanest integration — Bifrost's `/anthropic` native endpoint means the Claude Agent SDK works unchanged, and we eliminate the custom proxy code. The only risk is validating Bifrost handles streaming + tool use correctly, which we can verify in testing.

     1→import logging
     2→import uuid
     3→from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
     4→
     5→from fastapi import APIRouter, HTTPException, Request
     6→from fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse
     7→import httpx
     8→
     9→from app.config import ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
    10→
    11→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    12→
    13→ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM = "https://api.anthropic.com"
    14→
    15→_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {
    16→    "host", "content-length", "transfer-encoding", "connection",
    17→    "x-api-key",         # client must not override billing identity
    18→    "anthropic-version", # proxy controls API version
    19→    # anthropic-beta is intentionally NOT blocked: Claude Code sends beta body
    20→    # fields (e.g. context_management) alongside the matching beta header, and
    21→    # Anthropic rejects those fields as "extra inputs" if the header is absent.
    22→}
    23→_SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS = {"transfer-encoding", "content-encoding", "connection"}
    24→
    25→
    26→class ProxyTokenStore:
    27→    def __init__(self, ttl_seconds: int = 600):
    28→        self._ttl = ttl_seconds
    29→        self._tokens: dict[str, datetime] = {}
    30→
    31→    def create_token(self) -> str:
    32→        token = str(uuid.uuid4())
    33→        self._tokens[token] = datetime.now(timezone.utc) + timedelta(seconds=self._ttl)
    34→        return token
    35→
    36→    def validate_token(self, token: str) -> bool:
    37→        # Tokens are intentionally multi-use: Claude Code makes many API calls
    38→        # per session, all protected by the same UUID. The token is valid for
    39→        # the session TTL (600s safety net) but is explicitly revoked by
    40→        # proxy_token_store.revoke_token() in agent.py's finally block when
    41→        # the Claude Code subprocess exits. Do not make single-use.
    42→        expiry = self._tokens.get(token)
    43→        if expiry is None:
    44→            return False
    45→        if datetime.now(timezone.utc) > expiry:
    46→            self._tokens.pop(token, None)
    47→            return False
    48→        return True
    49→
    50→    def revoke_token(self, token: str) -> None:
    51→        self._tokens.pop(token, None)
    52→
    53→    def cleanup_expired(self) -> int:
    54→        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
    55→        expired = [t for t, exp in self._tokens.items() if now > exp]
    56→        for t in expired:
    57→            del self._tokens[t]
    58→        return len(expired)
    59→
    60→
    61→proxy_token_store = ProxyTokenStore()
    62→
    63→router = APIRouter(prefix="/anthropic")
    64→
    65→
    66→# Read-only discovery endpoints that the Claude Code SDK calls during startup
    67→# (e.g. GET /v1/models to resolve short model aliases like "sonnet").
    68→# These carry no session-specific data, so we forward them with the real API
    69→# key without requiring a registered session token.
    70→_UNAUTHENTICATED_PASSTHROUGH = {"v1/models"}
    71→
    72→
    73→@router.api_route("/{path:path}", methods=["GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "PATCH"])
    74→async def proxy_anthropic(path: str, request: Request):
    75→    # Allow a small set of read-only discovery endpoints to pass through
    76→    # without a session token so the SDK can resolve short model names.
    77→    if path not in _UNAUTHENTICATED_PASSTHROUGH:
    78→        # Claude Code CLI sends the API key as x-api-key (Anthropic SDK default).
    79→        # Fall back to Authorization: Bearer for other clients.
    80→        session_token = (
    81→            request.headers.get("x-api-key")
    82→            or request.headers.get("authorization", "")[len("Bearer "):]
    83→            or None
    84→        )
    85→        if not session_token or not proxy_token_store.validate_token(session_token):
    86→            raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid or expired session token")
    87→
    88→    headers = {
    89→        k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()
    90→        if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS
    91→    }
    92→    headers["x-api-key"] = ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
    93→    headers["anthropic-version"] = "2023-06-01"
    94→
    95→    body = await request.body()
    96→
    97→    # Use a fresh client per request: streaming responses that are cut short
    98→    # (e.g. client disconnects) leave connections in a partial read state, and
    99→    # reusing a shared SSL context across those connections causes
   100→    # SSLV3_ALERT_BAD_RECORD_MAC / record layer failure errors.
   101→    # Query params are intentionally NOT forwarded — Claude Code appends
   102→    # internal params (e.g. ?beta=true) that Anthropic rejects as "extra inputs".
   103→    client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))
   104→    try:
   105→        upstream_req = client.build_request(
   106→            method=request.method,
   107→            url=f"{ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM}/{path}",
   108→            headers=headers,
   109→            content=body,
   110→        )
   111→        upstream_resp = await client.send(upstream_req, stream=True)
   112→
   113→        response_headers = {
   114→            k: v for k, v in upstream_resp.headers.items()
   115→            if k.lower() not in _SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS
   116→        }
   117→
   118→        async def body_generator():
   119→            try:
   120→                async for chunk in upstream_resp.aiter_bytes():
   121→                    yield chunk
   122→            finally:
   123→                await upstream_resp.aclose()
   124→                await client.aclose()
   125→
   126→        return StreamingResponse(
   127→            body_generator(),
   128→            status_code=upstream_resp.status_code,
   129→            headers=response_headers,
   130→        )
   131→    except Exception:
   132→        await client.aclose()
   133→        logger.exception("Proxy request to upstream failed: %s %s", request.method, path)
   134→        raise
   135→
```

---

## Design Section 1: Architecture Overview

**Current flow:**
```
Sidecar → proxy.py (UUID token → real Anthropic key) → api.anthropic.com
```

**New flow:**
```
Sidecar → Bifrost (/anthropic endpoint) → Anthropic / OpenAI / Bedrock / etc.
```

**Key changes:**
1. **New `bifrost` service** in `docker-compose.yml` on the `agent-sandbox` network
2. **Remove `proxy.py`** and `ProxyTokenStore` entirely
3. **Sidecar env vars** change from `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://app:10000/anthropic` to `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://bifrost:8080/anthropic`
4. **No sidecar code changes** — the Claude Agent SDK already accepts `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, and Bifrost's `/anthropic` endpoint speaks native Anthropic Messages API
5. **API keys** move from `backend/.env` to Bifrost's `config.json` (or env vars passed to Bifrost)
6. **Model names** may use Bifrost prefixes (e.g., `openai/gpt-4o-mini` for subagents) — need to verify the SDK passes them through

Does this architecture look right so far?

> AGENT

Great question. Let me clarify the security model.

**With Bifrost, sidecar containers still never see real API keys.** Here's how:

1. **Bifrost stores the real keys** in its `config.json` (or via env vars). These are only accessible inside the Bifrost container.
2. **Sidecar gets a placeholder** — the Claude Agent SDK requires `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` to be set, but we can set it to any dummy value (e.g., `"placeholder"`). Bifrost is configured to use its own stored keys and ignores client-sent keys.
3. **Network isolation** — Bifrost is on the `agent-sandbox` Docker network. Sidecar containers can reach Bifrost, but Bifrost's `config.json` (with real keys) is inside the Bifrost container's filesystem, inaccessible to sidecars.

**Optional: Bifrost Virtual Keys** — Bifrost supports virtual keys (`x-bf-vk` header) for per-session or per-team access control. We could generate a virtual key per session (similar to current UUID tokens) for audit/rate-limiting. But even without this, the sidecar can't extract the real API key from Bifrost.

So the security posture is equivalent to (or better than) the current proxy pattern — real keys never leave the gateway.

## Design Section 2: Docker Compose & Configuration

**New `bifrost` service in `docker-compose.yml`:**

```yaml
services:
  bifrost:
    image: maximhq/bifrost:latest
    container_name: bifrost
    ports:
      - "${BIFROST_PORT:-8081}:8080"   # Web UI accessible from host
    volumes:
      - ./bifrost/config.json:/app/data/config.json  # Provider config
    environment:
      APP_HOST: "0.0.0.0"
      ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: ${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}  # Passed as env var, referenced in config.json
    networks:
      - agent-sandbox
    restart: unless-stopped
```

**New `bifrost/config.json`:**
```json
{
  "providers": {
    "anthropic": {
      "keys": [{ "name": "default", "value": "env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", "weight": 1.0 }]
    }
  }
}
```

Users can later add OpenAI, Bedrock, etc. by editing this file or via the Web UI.

**Changes to `app` service:**
- Remove `PROXY_BASE_URL` environment variable
- Add `BIFROST_BASE_URL: http://bifrost:8080` (new env var)
- `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` still needed in `backend/.env` only to pass to Bifrost via Docker Compose

**Changes to `backend/app/config.py`:**
- Replace `PROXY_BASE_URL` with `BIFROST_BASE_URL`
- Keep `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL`, `CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL`
- `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` can be removed from config.py (no longer used by backend directly)

Does this configuration approach look good?

## Design Section 3: Code Changes

### Files to modify:

**1. `backend/app/agent.py`** — The biggest change
- Remove `proxy_token_store` import and all token create/validate/revoke logic
- Replace `PROXY_BASE_URL` with `BIFROST_BASE_URL`
- Sidecar env vars become:
  ```python
  "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": "placeholder",  # Bifrost manages real keys
  "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{BIFROST_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
  ```
- MCP server URL: `f"{BIFROST_BASE_URL}/.."` — wait, MCP SSE is served by the backend, not Bifrost. So we need to keep the backend URL for MCP:
  ```python
  "mcp_server_url": f"http://duckdb-data-agent:10000/mcp/sse?session_id={stable_session}",
  ```
  This means we need **two URLs**: one for Bifrost (LLM API), one for backend (MCP). We'll introduce `BACKEND_BASE_URL` for MCP routing.

**2. `backend/app/config.py`**
- Remove `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (no longer used by backend)
- Replace `PROXY_BASE_URL` with `BIFROST_BASE_URL` (default: `http://bifrost:8080`)
- Add `BACKEND_BASE_URL` (default: `http://duckdb-data-agent:10000`) for MCP SSE

**3. `backend/app/main.py`**
- Remove `proxy_module` import and router inclusion
- Remove proxy token cleanup from background task

**4. `backend/app/proxy.py`**
- **Delete entirely**

**5. `docker-compose.yml`**
- Add `bifrost` service
- Update `app` environment variables

**6. New file: `bifrost/config.json`**
- Provider configuration

### Files unchanged:
- `sidecar/src/server.ts` — no changes needed (it reads `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` from env)
- `backend/app/mcp_sse.py` — unchanged
- `backend/app/container_manager.py` — unchanged
- Frontend — unchanged

## Design Section 4: Error Handling & Fallback

**Bifrost health check:**
- Bifrost exposes `/health` endpoint. The `app` service can use a Docker Compose `depends_on` with `condition: service_healthy` to ensure Bifrost is ready before the backend starts.

```yaml
bifrost:
  healthcheck:
    test: ["CMD", "wget", "--spider", "-q", "http://localhost:8080/health"]
    interval: 10s
    timeout: 5s
    retries: 3

app:
  depends_on:
    bifrost:
      condition: service_healthy
```

**Provider failover:**
- Bifrost has built-in failover. If Anthropic is down, it can route to a configured fallback provider — this is a Bifrost config concern, not a code change.

**Error propagation:**
- Bifrost forwards upstream HTTP errors transparently. The sidecar already handles API errors from the Claude Agent SDK, so no changes needed.

**Startup order:**
- Bifrost must be ready before the app spawns sidecar containers. The `depends_on` + healthcheck handles this.

---

## Design Section 5: Testing Strategy

1. **Smoke test:** Start the stack with `docker compose up`, verify Bifrost Web UI is accessible, send a chat message, confirm response flows through Bifrost
2. **Multi-provider test:** Configure an OpenAI key in Bifrost, set `REDACTED`, verify the subagent uses GPT-4o-mini via Bifrost
3. **Streaming test:** Verify SSE streaming works end-to-end through Bifrost (the `/anthropic` endpoint must support streaming)
4. **Tool use test:** Verify Claude Agent SDK tool calls (MCP tools) work correctly when routed through Bifrost
5. **Existing E2E tests:** Run the Playwright E2E test suite to verify no regressions

  "content": "# Design: Bifrost LLM Gateway Integration\n\n**Date:** 2026-02-26\n**Status:** Approved\n\n## Motivation\n\nReplace the custom credential proxy (`proxy.py`) with [Bifrost](https://github.com/maximhq/bifrost), a high-performance open-source LLM gateway, to:\n\n1. **Multi-provider support** — Route orchestrator and subagent models to any provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, Bedrock, Vertex, etc.) via Bifrost's prefix-based routing (e.g., `openai/gpt-4o-mini`)\n2. **Operational benefits** — Centralized key management, weighted load balancing across API keys, automatic failover, cost tracking via Bifrost's Web UI\n3. **Simplified architecture** — Remove the custom `ProxyTokenStore` and `proxy.py` in favor of Bifrost's battle-tested gateway\n\n## Architecture\n\n### Current Flow\n\n```\nSidecar Container\n  → proxy.py (UUID token validation → inject real ANTHROPIC_API_KEY)\n  → api.anthropic.com\n```\n\n### New Flow\n\n```\nSidecar Container\n  → Bifrost /anthropic endpoint (native Anthropic Messages API)\n  → Anthropic / OpenAI / Bedrock / etc.\n```\n\nKey architectural decisions:\n\n- **Keep Claude Agent SDK unchanged** — Bifrost's `/anthropic` endpoint speaks native Anthropic Messages API, so the TypeScript SDK in the sidecar requires no code changes\n- **Bifrost manages all API keys** — Real provider keys are stored in Bifrost's `config.json`, never exposed to sidecar containers\n- **Sidecar gets a placeholder key** — `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=placeholder` (Bifrost ignores client-sent keys and uses its own)\n- **Two URL paths** — `BIFROST_BASE_URL` for LLM API routing, `BACKEND_BASE_URL` for MCP SSE (MCP is served by the backend, not Bifrost)\n\n### Security Model\n\nEquivalent to the current proxy pattern:\n\n| Concern | Current (proxy.py) | New (Bifrost) |\n|---------|-------------------|---------------|\n| Real API key storage | Backend process memory | Bifrost container filesystem |\n| Sidecar credential | UUID session token | Placeholder string |\n| Key injection | proxy.py swaps UUID → real key | Bifrost injects stored key |\n| Key exposure to sidecar | Never | Never |\n\nBifrost additionally supports virtual keys (`x-bf-vk` header) for per-session/per-team access control, which can be adopted later if needed.\n\n## Docker Compose Changes\n\n### New `bifrost` service\n\n```yaml\nbifrost:\n  image: maximhq/bifrost:latest\n  container_name: bifrost\n  ports:\n    - \"${BIFROST_PORT:-8081}:8080\"\n  volumes:\n    - ./bifrost/config.json:/app/data/config.json\n  environment:\n    APP_HOST: \"0.0.0.0\"\n    ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: ${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}\n  networks:\n    - agent-sandbox\n  healthcheck:\n    test: [\"CMD\", \"wget\", \"--spider\", \"-q\", \"http://localhost:8080/health\"]\n    interval: 10s\n    timeout: 5s\n    retries: 3\n  restart: unless-stopped\n```\n\n### Updated `app` service\n\n```yaml\napp:\n  depends_on:\n    bifrost:\n      condition: service_healthy\n  environment:\n    BIFROST_BASE_URL: http://bifrost:8080\n    BACKEND_BASE_URL: http://duckdb-data-agent:10000\n    CONTAINER_IMAGE: duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest\n    CONTAINER_NETWORK: agent-sandbox\n```\n\nRemove: `PROXY_BASE_URL`\n\n### New `bifrost/config.json`\n\n```json\n{\n  \"providers\": {\n    \"anthropic\": {\n      \"keys\": [\n        {\n          \"name\": \"default\",\n          \"value\": \"env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY\",\n          \"models\": [],\n          \"weight\": 1.0\n        }\n      ]\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nAdditional providers (OpenAI, Bedrock, etc.) can be added to this file or via the Bifrost Web UI at `http://localhost:8081`.\n\n## Code Changes\n\n### Files to modify\n\n**`backend/app/config.py`**\n- Remove: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `PROXY_BASE_URL`\n- Add: `BIFROST_BASE_URL` (default: `http://bifrost:8080`)\n- Add: `BACKEND_BASE_URL` (default: `http://duckdb-data-agent:10000`)\n- Keep: `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL`, `CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL`\n\n**`backend/app/agent.py`**\n- Remove: `proxy_token_store` import, token create/revoke logic, `_delayed_revoke()`\n- Replace sidecar env vars:\n  ```python\n  # Before\n  \"ANTHROPIC_API_KEY\": session_token,\n  \"ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL\": f\"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic\",\n\n  # After\n  \"ANTHROPIC_API_KEY\": \"placeholder\",\n  \"ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL\": f\"{BIFROST_BASE_URL}/anthropic\",\n  ```\n- Replace MCP URL:\n  ```python\n  # Before\n  \"mcp_server_url\": f\"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/mcp/sse?session_id={stable_session}\",\n\n  # After\n  \"mcp_server_url\": f\"{BACKEND_BASE_URL}/mcp/sse?session_id={stable_session}\",\n  ```\n- Remove the `finally` block's token revocation logic\n\n**`backend/app/main.py`**\n- Remove: `proxy_module` import, `app.include_router(proxy_module.router)`, proxy token cleanup in background task\n\n### Files to delete\n\n**`backend/app/proxy.py`** — Entire file removed (ProxyTokenStore + proxy router)\n\n### Files to add\n\n**`bifrost/config.json`** — Bifrost provider configuration\n\n### Files unchanged\n\n- `sidecar/src/server.ts` — Reads `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` from env; no changes needed\n- `sidecar/src/types.ts` — No changes\n- `backend/app/mcp_sse.py` — MCP still served by backend\n- `backend/app/container_manager.py` — No changes\n- Frontend — No changes\n\n## Error Handling\n\n- **Startup order**: `app` depends on `bifrost` with `condition: service_healthy`; Bifrost healthcheck ensures it's ready before the backend starts spawning sidecars\n- **Provider failover**: Bifrost has built-in failover across providers; configured in `config.json`, not in application code\n- **Error propagation**: Bifrost forwards upstream HTTP errors transparently; sidecar's existing error handling in the Claude Agent SDK remains effective\n\n## Testing Strategy\n\n1. **Smoke test** — `docker compose up`, verify Bifrost Web UI at `:8081`, send a chat message, confirm response flows through Bifrost\n2. **Multi-provider test** — Add OpenAI key to Bifrost config, set `REDACTED`, verify subagent uses GPT-4o-mini\n3. **Streaming test** — Verify SSE streaming works end-to-end through Bifrost's `/anthropic` endpoint\n4. **Tool use test** — Verify Claude Agent SDK tool calls (MCP tools) work correctly when routed through Bifrost\n5. **E2E regression** — Run existing Playwright E2E test suite to verify no regressions\n\n## Multi-Provider Model Configuration\n\nWith Bifrost, model names can use provider prefixes:\n\n| Config Variable | Example Values |\n|----------------|----------------|\n| `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` | `claude-sonnet-4-6`, `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6` |\n| `SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL` | `haiku`, `openai/gpt-4o-mini`, `bedrock/claude-3-haiku` |\n| `CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL` | `haiku`, `openai/gpt-4o-mini` |\n\nBifrost routes based on the prefix. Unprefixed names default to the Anthropic provider.\n"
}
```

     1→# Design: Bifrost LLM Gateway Integration
     2→
     3→**Date:** 2026-02-26
     4→**Status:** Approved
     5→
     6→## Motivation
     7→
     8→Replace the custom credential proxy (`proxy.py`) with [Bifrost](https://github.com/maximhq/bifrost), a high-performance open-source LLM gateway, to:
     9→
    10→1. **Multi-provider support** — Route orchestrator and subagent models to any provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, Bedrock, Vertex, etc.) via Bifrost's prefix-based routing (e.g., `openai/gpt-4o-mini`)
    11→2. **Operational benefits** — Centralized key management, weighted load balancing across API keys, automatic failover, cost tracking via Bifrost's Web UI
    12→3. **Simplified architecture** — Remove the custom `ProxyTokenStore` and `proxy.py` in favor of Bifrost's battle-tested gateway
    13→
    14→## Architecture
    15→
    16→### Current Flow
    17→
    18→```
    19→Sidecar Container
    20→  → proxy.py (UUID token validation → inject real ANTHROPIC_API_KEY)
    21→  → api.anthropic.com
    22→```
    23→
    24→### New Flow
    25→
    26→```
    27→Sidecar Container
    28→  → Bifrost /anthropic endpoint (native Anthropic Messages API)
    29→  → Anthropic / OpenAI / Bedrock / etc.
    30→```
    31→
    32→Key architectural decisions:
    33→
    34→- **Keep Claude Agent SDK unchanged** — Bifrost's `/anthropic` endpoint speaks native Anthropic Messages API, so the TypeScript SDK in the sidecar requires no code changes
    35→- **Bifrost manages all API keys** — Real provider keys are stored in Bifrost's `config.json`, never exposed to sidecar containers
    36→- **Sidecar gets a placeholder key** — `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=placeholder` (Bifrost ignores client-sent keys and uses its own)
    37→- **Two URL paths** — `BIFROST_BASE_URL` for LLM API routing, `BACKEND_BASE_URL` for MCP SSE (MCP is served by the backend, not Bifrost)
    38→
    39→### Security Model
    40→
    41→Equivalent to the current proxy pattern:
    42→
    43→| Concern | Current (proxy.py) | New (Bifrost) |
    44→|---------|-------------------|---------------|
    45→| Real API key storage | Backend process memory | Bifrost container filesystem |
    46→| Sidecar credential | UUID session token | Placeholder string |
    47→| Key injection | proxy.py swaps UUID → real key | Bifrost injects stored key |
    48→| Key exposure to sidecar | Never | Never |
    49→
    50→Bifrost additionally supports virtual keys (`x-bf-vk` header) for per-session/per-team access control, which can be adopted later if needed.
    51→
    52→## Docker Compose Changes
    53→
    54→### New `bifrost` service
    55→
    56→```yaml
    57→bifrost:
    58→  image: maximhq/bifrost:latest
    59→  container_name: bifrost
    60→  ports:
    61→    - "${BIFROST_PORT:-8081}:8080"
    62→  volumes:
    63→    - ./bifrost/config.json:/app/data/config.json
    64→  environment:
    65→    APP_HOST: "0.0.0.0"
    66→    ANTHROPIC_API_KEY: ${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}
    67→  networks:
    68→    - agent-sandbox
    69→  healthcheck:
    70→    test: ["CMD", "wget", "--spider", "-q", "http://localhost:8080/health"]
    71→    interval: 10s
    72→    timeout: 5s
    73→    retries: 3
    74→  restart: unless-stopped
    75→```
    76→
    77→### Updated `app` service
    78→
    79→```yaml
    80→app:
    81→  depends_on:
    82→    bifrost:
    83→      condition: service_healthy
    84→  environment:
    85→    BIFROST_BASE_URL: http://bifrost:8080
    86→    BACKEND_BASE_URL: http://duckdb-data-agent:10000
    87→    CONTAINER_IMAGE: duckdb-agent-sidecar:latest
    88→    CONTAINER_NETWORK: agent-sandbox
    89→```
    90→
    91→Remove: `PROXY_BASE_URL`
    92→
    93→### New `bifrost/config.json`
    94→
    95→```json
    96→{
    97→  "providers": {
    98→    "anthropic": {
    99→      "keys": [
   100→        {
   101→          "name": "default",
   102→          "value": "env.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY",
   103→          "models": [],
   104→          "weight": 1.0
   105→        }
   106→      ]
   107→    }
   108→  }
   109→}
   110→```
   111→
   112→Additional providers (OpenAI, Bedrock, etc.) can be added to this file or via the Bifrost Web UI at `http://localhost:8081`.
   113→
   114→## Code Changes
   115→
   116→### Files to modify
   117→
   118→**`backend/app/config.py`**
   119→- Remove: `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `PROXY_BASE_URL`
   120→- Add: `BIFROST_BASE_URL` (default: `http://bifrost:8080`)
   121→- Add: `BACKEND_BASE_URL` (default: `http://duckdb-data-agent:10000`)
   122→- Keep: `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL`, `CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL`
   123→
   124→**`backend/app/agent.py`**
   125→- Remove: `proxy_token_store` import, token create/revoke logic, `_delayed_revoke()`
   126→- Replace sidecar env vars:
   127→  ```python
   128→  # Before
   129→  "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": session_token,
   130→  "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
   131→
   132→  # After
   133→  "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": "placeholder",
   134→  "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{BIFROST_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
   135→  ```
   136→- Replace MCP URL:
   137→  ```python
   138→  # Before
   139→  "mcp_server_url": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/mcp/sse?session_id={stable_session}",
   140→
   141→  # After
   142→  "mcp_server_url": f"{BACKEND_BASE_URL}/mcp/sse?session_id={stable_session}",
   143→  ```
   144→- Remove the `finally` block's token revocation logic
   145→
   146→**`backend/app/main.py`**
   147→- Remove: `proxy_module` import, `app.include_router(proxy_module.router)`, proxy token cleanup in background task
   148→
   149→### Files to delete
   150→
   151→**`backend/app/proxy.py`** — Entire file removed (ProxyTokenStore + proxy router)
   152→
   153→### Files to add
   154→
   155→**`bifrost/config.json`** — Bifrost provider configuration
   156→
   157→### Files unchanged
   158→
   159→- `sidecar/src/server.ts` — Reads `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` from env; no changes needed
   160→- `sidecar/src/types.ts` — No changes
   161→- `backend/app/mcp_sse.py` — MCP still served by backend
   162→- `backend/app/container_manager.py` — No changes
   163→- Frontend — No changes
   164→
   165→## Error Handling
   166→
   167→- **Startup order**: `app` depends on `bifrost` with `condition: service_healthy`; Bifrost healthcheck ensures it's ready before the backend starts spawning sidecars
   168→- **Provider failover**: Bifrost has built-in failover across providers; configured in `config.json`, not in application code
   169→- **Error propagation**: Bifrost forwards upstream HTTP errors transparently; sidecar's existing error handling in the Claude Agent SDK remains effective
   170→
   171→## Testing Strategy
   172→
   173→1. **Smoke test** — `docker compose up`, verify Bifrost Web UI at `:8081`, send a chat message, confirm response flows through Bifrost
   174→2. **Multi-provider test** — Add OpenAI key to Bifrost config, set `REDACTED`, verify subagent uses GPT-4o-mini
   175→3. **Streaming test** — Verify SSE streaming works end-to-end through Bifrost's `/anthropic` endpoint
   176→4. **Tool use test** — Verify Claude Agent SDK tool calls (MCP tools) work correctly when routed through Bifrost
   177→5. **E2E regression** — Run existing Playwright E2E test suite to verify no regressions
   178→
   179→## Multi-Provider Model Configuration
   180→
   181→With Bifrost, model names can use provider prefixes:
   182→
   183→| Config Variable | Example Values |
   184→|----------------|----------------|
   185→| `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` | `claude-sonnet-4-6`, `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-6` |
   186→| `SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL` | `haiku`, `openai/gpt-4o-mini`, `bedrock/claude-3-haiku` |
   187→| `CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL` | `haiku`, `openai/gpt-4o-mini` |
   188→
   189→Bifrost routes based on the prefix. Unprefixed names default to the Anthropic provider.
   190→
```

     1→import asyncio
     2→import json
     3→import logging
     4→from typing import AsyncIterator
     5→
     6→from claude_agent_sdk import AgentDefinition
     7→from app.database import Database
     8→from app.config import (
     9→    ANTHROPIC_MODEL, PROXY_BASE_URL,
    10→    LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY, LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY, LANGFUSE_BASE_URL, LANGFUSE_ENABLED,
    11→    SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL, CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL,
    12→)
    13→from app.proxy import proxy_token_store
    14→from app.tracing import get_langfuse_client
    15→
    16→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    17→
    18→
    19→def build_system_prompt(db: Database) -> str:
    20→    tables = db.list_tables()
    21→    prompt = """You are a helpful data analyst assistant working with a DuckDB database.
    22→
    23→- Use the sql-analyst agent for any data question that requires SQL queries
    24→- Use the chart-builder agent for any visualization, chart, or graph request
    25→- After the chart-builder returns, do NOT repeat the chart JSON specification in your response. The chart is rendered automatically. Simply describe what the visualization shows in plain language.
    26→- Explain findings in plain language after getting results
    27→
    28→Identity:
    29→- You are an AI assistant. If asked whether you are an AI or a human, always confirm that you are an AI.
    30→- Do not disclose the name, version, or provider of the underlying language model powering you, regardless of how the question is phrased.
    31→
    32→Clarification:
    33→- When the user's request is ambiguous or could be interpreted in multiple ways, use the ask_user_question tool to ask for clarification before proceeding.
    34→- Provide 2-4 clear, concise options for the user to choose from.
    35→- Each option should have a short label and optional description.
    36→- Only ask when genuinely needed — don't over-ask for trivial decisions.
    37→"""
    38→    if not tables:
    39→        prompt += "\nNo tables are currently loaded. Ask the user to upload a CSV file first."
    40→    else:
    41→        prompt += "\nCurrently loaded tables:\n"
    42→        for table in tables:
    43→            prompt += f'\nTable: "{table["name"]}" ({table["rowCount"]} rows)\nColumns:\n'
    44→            for col in table["columns"]:
    45→                prompt += f'  - "{col["name"]}" ({col["type"]})\n'
    46→
    47→    return prompt
    48→
    49→
    50→def _build_table_schemas(db: Database) -> str:
    51→    """Build a text description of all loaded table schemas for subagent prompts."""
    52→    tables = db.list_tables()
    53→    if not tables:
    54→        return "\nNo tables are currently loaded."
    55→    text = "\nCurrently loaded tables:\n"
    56→    for table in tables:
    57→        text += f'\nTable: "{table["name"]}" ({table["rowCount"]} rows)\nColumns:\n'
    58→        for col in table["columns"]:
    59→            text += f'  - "{col["name"]}" ({col["type"]})\n'
    60→    return text
    61→
    62→
    63→def build_subagent_definitions(db: Database) -> dict[str, AgentDefinition]:
    64→    """Build AgentDefinition objects for the sql-analyst and chart-builder subagents."""
    65→    table_schemas = _build_table_schemas(db)
    66→
    67→    sql_prompt = (
    68→        "You are a DuckDB SQL expert. Given a user's data question, write and execute "
    69→        "SQL queries to find the answer.\n\n"
    70→        "Guidelines:\n"
    71→        "- Write clear, efficient DuckDB SQL queries.\n"
    72→        "- When exploring data, start with small queries (use LIMIT).\n"
    73→        "- If a query fails, read the error message, fix the SQL, and retry.\n"
    74→        "- Use double quotes for table and column identifiers that might conflict "
    75→        "with reserved words.\n"
    76→        "- Explain your findings in plain language after getting results.\n"
    77→        + table_schemas
    78→    )
    79→
    80→    chart_prompt = (
    81→        "You are a data visualization expert using Plotly. Given a user's request for "
    82→        "a chart or visualization, query the data with SQL and then call the "
    83→        "`render_chart` tool with the Plotly spec.\n\n"
    84→        "Guidelines:\n"
    85→        "- Choose the most appropriate chart type (bar, line, scatter, pie, histogram, "
    86→        "box, heatmap, etc.).\n"
    87→        "- For pie charts, use `labels` and `values` fields in the trace.\n"
    88→        "- For multi-series data, group into separate traces.\n"
    89→        "- `layout.title` is required — always provide a descriptive title.\n"
    90→        "- Keep the chart clean and readable.\n\n"
    91→        "IMPORTANT — keep data small. Pre-aggregate in SQL instead of passing raw rows:\n"
    92→        "- Box plots: compute lowerfence, q1, median, q3, upperfence per group in SQL. "
    93→        "Use trace type \"box\" with those pre-computed fields instead of a raw `y` array.\n"
    94→        "- Histograms: compute bin counts with width_bucket() or CASE in SQL, then "
    95→        "render as a bar chart with the bin edges as `x` and counts as `y`.\n"
    96→        "- Scatter / line with many rows: sample (ORDER BY random() LIMIT 200) or "
    97→        "aggregate (e.g. average per time bucket) so each trace has at most ~200 points.\n"
    98→        "- General rule: each trace should have at most ~200 data points. "
    99→        "Large tool-call inputs cause malformed JSON and validation errors.\n\n"
   100→        "When ready to render, call `render_chart` with:\n"
   101→        "- `data`: array of Plotly trace objects\n"
   102→        "- `layout`: object containing at minimum {\"title\": \"<descriptive title>\"}\n\n"
   103→        "Do NOT output a JSON code block. Use the tool.\n"
   104→        + table_schemas
   105→    )
   106→
   107→    return {
   108→        "sql-analyst": AgentDefinition(
   109→            description=(
   110→                "Use this agent for any data question that requires SQL queries "
   111→                "— exploring data, aggregations, filtering, joins, etc."
   112→            ),
   113→            prompt=sql_prompt,
   114→            tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
   115→            model=SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL,
   116→        ),
   117→        "chart-builder": AgentDefinition(
   118→            description=(
   119→                "Use this agent when the user wants a chart, graph, or visualization."
   120→            ),
   121→            prompt=chart_prompt,
   122→            tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql", "mcp__duckdb__render_chart"],
   123→            model=CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL,
   124→        ),
   125→    }
   126→
   127→
   128→def _build_message_with_history(
   129→    message: str, conversation_history: list[dict] | None = None
   130→) -> str:
   131→    """Prepend conversation history context to the user message when editing."""
   132→    if not conversation_history:
   133→        return message
   134→
   135→    history_text = "Previous conversation (for context, I am now editing a message):\n"
   136→    for entry in conversation_history:
   137→        role = entry.get("role", "user").capitalize()
   138→        content = entry.get("content", "")
   139→        history_text += f"\n{role}: {content}\n"
   140→    history_text += f"\n---\n\nMy updated message:\n{message}"
   141→    return history_text
   142→
   143→
   144→
   145→def _build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages(
   146→    msg: dict, tool_names: dict[str, str], subagent_chart_specs: dict
   147→) -> None:
   148→    """Inspect an assistant message and capture render_chart input as chart_spec.
   149→
   150→    When the chart-builder subagent calls render_chart, the tool_use block
   151→    appears in an assistant message whose parent_tool_use_id is the Task
   152→    tool_use_id for the chart-builder.  We extract input directly so we never
   153→    need to parse free-text JSON.
   154→    """
   155→    parent_id = msg.get("parent_tool_use_id")
   156→    if not parent_id or tool_names.get(parent_id) != "chart-builder":
   157→        return
   158→    for block in msg.get("message", {}).get("content", []):
   159→        if block.get("type") == "tool_use" and "render_chart" in block.get("name", ""):
   160→            subagent_chart_specs[parent_id] = block.get("input", {})
   161→            return
   162→
   163→
   164→async def stream_chat(
   165→    message: str,
   166→    session_id: str | None = None,
   167→    db: Database | None = None,
   168→    conversation_history: list[dict] | None = None,
   169→    langfuse_session_id: str | None = None,
   170→    backend_session_id: str | None = None,
   171→) -> AsyncIterator[str]:
   172→    """Stream agent chat responses as SSE events via containerized sidecar."""
   173→    from app.container_manager import container_manager
   174→    if container_manager is None:
   175→        raise RuntimeError("Docker is not available. Container mode requires Docker.")
   176→    if db is None:
   177→        raise ValueError("db must be provided")
   178→
   179→    import httpx
   180→    import asyncio
   181→
   182→    query_message = _build_message_with_history(message, conversation_history)
   183→    system_prompt = build_system_prompt(db)
   184→
   185→    session_token=[REDACTED].create_token()
   186→
   187→    # Pass Langfuse credentials to the container so the sidecar's
   188→    # TypeScript Langfuse SDK can create traces directly.
   189→    env: dict[str, str] = {
   190→        "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": session_token,
   191→        "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
   192→    }
   193→    if LANGFUSE_ENABLED:
   194→        env["LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY"] = LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY
   195→        env["LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY"] = LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY
   196→        env["LANGFUSE_BASE_URL"] = LANGFUSE_BASE_URL
   197→
   198→    if "127.0.0.1" in PROXY_BASE_URL or "localhost" in PROXY_BASE_URL:
   199→        logger.warning(
   200→            "PROXY_BASE_URL=%s uses localhost which is unreachable from containers. "
   201→            "Set PROXY_BASE_URL to the host's Docker-accessible address "
   202→            "(e.g., http://host.docker.internal:10000).",
   203→            PROXY_BASE_URL,
   204→        )
   205→
   206→    # Use the backend session ID (X-Session-ID header) for both:
   207→    # 1. MCP SSE URL — so the container queries the correct DuckDB instance
   208→    # 2. Container lifecycle key — so the same container is reused across
   209→    #    requests from the same browser tab (the Claude agent session_id
   210→    #    changes after the first response, which would orphan the container)
   211→    stable_session = backend_session_id or session_id or "default"
   212→
   213→    try:
   214→        # Send SSE keepalive immediately so the HTTP response starts and
   215→        # intermediate proxies (Vite, nginx) don't drop the idle connection
   216→        # before we've finished the blocking Docker container creation.
   217→        yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   218→
   219→        # container_manager.create() is synchronous (blocking Docker API call).
   220→        # Run it in a thread executor so the event loop stays responsive and
   221→        # can continue flushing keepalives to the client during startup.
   222→        # gVisor (runsc) containers can take 10-30 seconds to spin up.
   223→        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   224→        create_future = loop.run_in_executor(None, container_manager.create, stable_session, env)
   225→
   226→        max_create_wait = 60.0
   227→        elapsed = 0.0
   228→        while not create_future.done():
   229→            await asyncio.sleep(2.0)
   230→            elapsed += 2.0
   231→            if elapsed >= max_create_wait:
   232→                create_future.cancel()
   233→                raise RuntimeError(f"Container creation timed out after {max_create_wait:.0f}s")
   234→            yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   235→        info = await create_future
   236→
   237→        # Wait for container to be ready
   238→        for attempt in range(10):
   239→            try:
   240→                async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(5.0)) as check_client:
   241→                    resp = await check_client.get(f"{info.url}/health")
   242→                    if resp.status_code == 200:
   243→                        break
   244→            except Exception:
   245→                pass
   246→            yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   247→            await asyncio.sleep(1)
   248→        else:
   249→            raise RuntimeError("Sidecar container failed health check after 10 attempts")
   250→
   251→        payload: dict = {
   252→            "message": query_message,
   253→            "session_id": session_id,
   254→            "system_prompt": system_prompt,
   255→            "model": ANTHROPIC_MODEL,
   256→            "mcp_server_url": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/mcp/sse?session_id={stable_session}",
   257→            "env": {
   258→                "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": session_token,
   259→                "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{PROXY_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
   260→            },
   261→            "agents": {
   262→                name: {
   263→                    "description": agent_def.description,
   264→                    "prompt": agent_def.prompt,
   265→                    "tools": agent_def.tools,
   266→                    "model": agent_def.model,
   267→                }
   268→                for name, agent_def in build_subagent_definitions(db).items()
   269→            },
   270→        }
   271→        if langfuse_session_id:
   272→            payload["langfuse_session_id"] = langfuse_session_id
   273→        # Pass original message & history separately for Langfuse trace metadata
   274→        if conversation_history:
   275→            payload["original_message"] = message
   276→            payload["conversation_history"] = conversation_history
   277→
   278→        has_tool_calls = False
   279→        has_thinking = False
   280→        done_sent = False
   281→        waiting_for_user = False
   282→        tool_names: dict[str, str] = {}
   283→        tool_sqls: dict[str, str] = {}
   284→        # Track subagent text output from intermediate assistant messages.
   285→        # The TypeScript SDK's Task tool_result contains only metadata (agentId,
   286→        # usage), not the subagent's actual output.  The real output arrives in
   287→        # assistant messages whose parent_tool_use_id matches the Task tool ID.
   288→        subagent_texts: dict[str, str] = {}
   289→        subagent_chart_specs: dict[str, dict] = {}
   290→        actual_session_id = session_id
   291→
   292→        async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0)) as client:
   293→            async with client.stream("POST", f"{info.url}/query", json=payload) as response:
   294→                line_iter = response.aiter_lines().__aiter__()
   295→                while True:
   296→                    try:
   297→                        if waiting_for_user:
   298→                            try:
   299→                                line = await asyncio.wait_for(line_iter.__anext__(), timeout=5.0)
   300→                            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
   301→                                yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   302→                                continue
   303→                        else:
   304→                            line = await line_iter.__anext__()
   305→                    except StopAsyncIteration:
   306→                        break
   307→
   308→                    if not line.startswith("data: "):
   309→                        continue
   310→                    raw = line[6:]
   311→                    waiting_for_user = False
   312→                    try:
   313→                        msg = json.loads(raw)
   314→                    except json.JSONDecodeError:
   315→                        continue
   316→
   317→                    msg_type = msg.get("type")
   318→                    # --- Token-level streaming events from SDK ---
   319→                    if msg_type == "stream_event":
   320→                        event = msg.get("event", {})
   321→                        event_type = event.get("type", "")
   322→                        stream_parent = msg.get("parent_tool_use_id")
   323→                        is_subagent_event = bool(stream_parent and stream_parent in tool_names)
   324→
   325→                        if event_type == "content_block_delta":
   326→                            delta = event.get("delta", {})
   327→                            delta_type = delta.get("type", "")
   328→                            if delta_type == "thinking_delta":
   329→                                text = delta.get("thinking", "")
   330→                                if text and not is_subagent_event:
   331→                                    yield f"event: thinking\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   332→                            elif delta_type == "text_delta":
   333→                                text = delta.get("text", "")
   334→                                if text:
   335→                                    # Accumulate subagent text for chart_spec
   336→                                    # extraction (the SDK may not yield a
   337→                                    # complete assistant message for the
   338→                                    # subagent's final turn).
   339→                                    if is_subagent_event:
   340→                                        subagent_texts[stream_parent] = subagent_texts.get(stream_parent, "") + text
   341→                                    else:
   342→                                        event_name = "answer" if has_tool_calls else "thinking"
   343→                                        yield f"event: {event_name}\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   344→
   345→                        elif event_type == "content_block_start":
   346→                            block = event.get("content_block", {})
   347→                            block_type = block.get("type")
   348→                            if block_type == "thinking":
   349→                                if not is_subagent_event:
   350→                                    has_thinking = True
   351→                            elif block_type == "text":
   352→                                if has_thinking and not is_subagent_event:
   353→                                    yield f"event: thinking_done\ndata: {json.dumps({})}\n\n"
   354→                            elif block_type == "tool_use":
   355→                                if not is_subagent_event:
   356→                                    has_thinking = False
   357→                                    has_tool_calls = True
   358→
   359→                    # --- Complete assistant message (contains tool_use blocks) ---
   360→                    elif msg_type == "assistant":
   361→                        message_obj = msg.get("message", {})
   362→                        parent_tool_use_id = msg.get("parent_tool_use_id")
   363→
   364→                        # Capture text from subagent assistant messages.
   365→                        # These carry the real subagent output (e.g. chart JSON)
   366→                        # that the Task tool_result metadata lacks.
   367→                        if parent_tool_use_id and parent_tool_use_id in tool_names:
   368→                            text_parts = []
   369→                            for block in message_obj.get("content", []):
   370→                                if block.get("type") == "text" and block.get("text"):
   371→                                    text_parts.append(block["text"])
   372→                            if text_parts:
   373→                                subagent_texts[parent_tool_use_id] = "\n".join(text_parts)
   374→                        _build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages(msg, tool_names, subagent_chart_specs)
   375→
   376→                        for block in message_obj.get("content", []):
   377→                            block_type = block.get("type")
   378→                            if block_type == "tool_use":
   379→                                has_tool_calls = True
   380→                                tool_id = block.get("id", "")
   381→                                tool_name = block.get("name", "")
   382→                                tool_input = block.get("input", {})
   383→                                tool_names[tool_id] = tool_name
   384→                                is_execute_sql = "execute_sql" in tool_name
   385→                                sql = tool_input.get("sql", "") if is_execute_sql else ""
   386→                                if sql:
   387→                                    tool_sqls[tool_id] = sql
   388→                                tool_call_data: dict = {"id": tool_id, "name": tool_name}
   389→                                if sql:
   390→                                    tool_call_data["sql"] = sql
   391→                                else:
   392→                                    tool_call_data["input"] = tool_input
   393→                                yield f"event: tool_call\ndata: {json.dumps(tool_call_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   394→                                if tool_name == "Task":
   395→                                    subagent_name = tool_input.get("subagent_type", "unknown")
   396→                                    subagent_prompt = tool_input.get("prompt", "")
   397→                                    tool_names[tool_id] = subagent_name
   398→                                    yield f"event: subagent_start\ndata: {json.dumps({'id': tool_id, 'name': subagent_name, 'prompt': subagent_prompt})}\n\n"
   399→
   400→                                # Detect ask_user_question tool
   401→                                if "ask_user_question" in tool_name:
   402→                                    from app.pending_questions import pending_question_store
   403→                                    import asyncio as _asyncio
   404→                                    for _ in range(50):
   405→                                        pending = pending_question_store.get_pending(stable_session)
   406→                                        if pending:
   407→                                            yield f"event: user_question\ndata: {json.dumps({'question_id': pending['question_id'], **pending['data']})}\n\n"
   408→                                            waiting_for_user = True

   410→                                        await _asyncio.sleep(0.1)

   414→                        message_obj = msg.get("message", {})

   416→                        # tool_use_result.content (a list of content blocks).
   417→                        # Extract it so we can use it for chart_spec extraction.
   418→                        tool_use_result = msg.get("tool_use_result")
   419→                        tool_use_result_text = ""
   420→                        if isinstance(tool_use_result, dict):
   421→                            tur_content = tool_use_result.get("content")
   422→                            if isinstance(tur_content, list):
   423→                                parts = []
   424→                                for item in tur_content:
   425→                                    if isinstance(item, dict) and item.get("type") == "text":
   426→                                        parts.append(item.get("text", ""))
   427→                                tool_use_result_text = "\n".join(parts)

   432→                            name = tool_names.get(tool_id, "")

   435→                            if isinstance(content_parts, list):
   436→                                for part in content_parts:
   437→                                    if isinstance(part, dict) and part.get("type") == "text":
   438→                                        text = part.get("text", "")
   439→                            elif isinstance(content_parts, str):
   440→                                text = content_parts
   441→
   442→                            # Try to parse structured MCP result

   446→                            if original_sql:
   447→                                result_data["sql"] = original_sql

   449→                                parsed = json.loads(text)
   450→                                if parsed.get("status") == "success":
   451→                                    if "chart_spec" in parsed:
